# Credit Put Spreads — Daily Scan Log

Structured snapshot of the CPS tab per trading day. Sister log to `metrics-logs.md` (Naked Puts) and `daily-briefings.md` (narrative). One entry per scan, descending order.

Authoritative data lives in `cps_candidate_history` + `cps_scan_responses` tables; this file is the human-readable mirror for day-over-day pattern recognition (streak tracking, c/w drift, which tickers stay eligible).

---

## Update Protocol

**Trigger:** After updating `metrics-logs.md` with the day's Naked Puts data, paste the CPS tab into this file.

**Steps:**
1. Receive Credit Put Spreads tab screenshot or data from the user
2. Insert new entry **at the top** of the log (immediately below the `---` after this protocol section)
3. Use heading format: `## YYYY-MM-DD (Day of week)`
4. Capture three blocks per entry: **Scan summary**, **Overlay**, **Candidates table**

**Required fields:**
- **Scan summary** — `Checked X / N actionable / B base_gate / C construction / E execution / O overlay / F confirmation`
- **Overlay** — `VIX <v> / VIX3M <v3> / VVIX <vv> — <status>, <Contango|Backwardation>`
- **Candidates table** — # / Ticker / Action / Days / Score / C/W / Credit / Width / Max Loss / RV Status / Notes

**Optional fields:**
- `Notable:` one-liner ONLY when something is worth flagging (first long streak, first SELL_CPS, regime overlay change, etc.). Don't force it.

**Column order:**
```
| # | Ticker | Action | Days | Score | C/W | Credit | Width | Max Loss | RV Status | Notes |
```

**Action values:** `SELL` | `WATCH` (SELL_CPS / WATCH_CPS — the user-visible chip strings)
**RV Status values:** `Excellent` | `Good` | `Acceptable` | `Caution` | `Avoid / Wait`

---

> **IMPORTANT:** Entries are in **descending order** (newest first). New entries go immediately below this line.

---

## 2026-09-04 (Friday)

**Scan summary:** Checked 11 / 0 actionable / 10 base_gate / 1 construction / 0 execution / 0 overlay / 0 confirmation
**Overlay:** VIX 14.53 / VIX3M 17.61 / VVIX 84.4 — NORMAL, Contango

**Notable:** The board goes blank: **zero actionable, the first empty CPS tab of the run** — IWM's return dies at **2d**, XLF fails base gate again after its one-session cameo, and **base gate now culls 10 of 11** with construction holding the last name. No credit, no width, no skew to quote; the funnel is **checked 11 / actionable 0 / base_gate 10 / construction 1 / execution 0**, and execution is blank for the **seventy-sixth consecutive print**. The surface keeps grinding lower: **VIX 14.32 → 14.53** — nominally up but still inside the 14.43 low's orbit — **VIX3M 17.42 → 17.61**, slope re-steepening to **0.82**, **VVIX 83.8 → 84.4**, contango intact, NORMAL. That is the whole problem in three numbers: implied vol at the floor of the range with term structure widening, which prices back-month risk while paying nothing for the front. Internals confirm rather than contradict: **THE PLAYOFFS at 1S / 0C + 1W, 1 tradeable**, **danger 0**, but **stress worsens 3 → 6 (18.2%) of 33 eligible** and **avg VRP improves −5.0 → −4.1 while the negative share drops 66% → 55%** — the first two-sided move in a week, and still not one name with positive VRP worth selling. Day-over-day is mildly constructive off the bottom: **JNJ +18 to 47, XLI +17, XLB +16 off zero, SBUX +14, GS +6, PLTR +5, MCD +4** against **XLV −20, TSLA −10, HOOD −7, GLD/XLF −6**. The one fact that matters is **NKE +3 to 68 — third straight session of gains, top of the board, and now inside striking distance of the gate for the first time in the run**; it is the only name that has strung persistence together rather than spiking and reverting. The read: today was a nothing-print by construction, but the internals moved the right way for once — VRP less negative, negative share back under 60% — while the surface moved the wrong way, and a rising stress count with falling VIX is compression, not opportunity. **Still zero SELL_CPS at 76 sessions.** The trigger is unchanged and unmet: VIX holding above 16 across multiple sessions with VRP positive across a majority. NKE clearing 70 with the surface cooperating is the single most plausible path to a first ticket, and it is the only thing on this board worth watching Monday.

## 2026-09-03 (Thursday)

**Notable:** IWM holds the return alive at **2d, its first three-session run since the nine-day streak died, but the score fades 44 → 37 (−7)** on **$0.42 credit over a $3 width for $2.58 max loss** — a penny back off yesterday's $0.43, skew easing **14.3% → 14.2%**, RV still **Good**, "Thin premium" welded on for the eleventh straight appearance. XLF returns at **0d after one session off**, and it brings back the number that vanished on Tuesday: **34.0% c/w, exactly matching Monday's record — but on $0.17 credit over a $0.50 width for $0.33 max loss**, which is the whole problem with the skew clause stated in one line. Record-tying relative premium worth seventeen cents is a rounding error, not a ticket, and the two names now sit twenty points of skew apart — the widest divergence of the run, one session after the tightest. Funnel is **checked 11 / actionable 2 / base_gate 8 / construction 1 / execution 0** — base gate loosens by exactly one name for the first time in four days, and execution is blank for the **seventy-fifth consecutive print**. The surface keeps giving back Tuesday's break: **VIX 15.20 → 14.32, back within a tick of the 14.43 low; VIX3M 17.73 → 17.42, slope re-flattening to 0.819; VVIX 86.3 → 83.8, finally off the pin but in the wrong direction.** Internals mirror it: regime holds **THE PLAYOFFS at 1S / 0C + 2W with 1 tradeable**, **stress 7 → 3 (9.4%) of 32 eligible, danger 0**, RV accel 0.815 — but **avg VRP deteriorates −4.7 → −5.0 and the negative share worsens 61% → 66%**. Yesterday's flagged candidate did the predictable thing — **NKE +3 to 65, still two under the gate**, closest of the run and now two sessions of persistence, the only genuinely constructive fact on the board. The read: vol compressed, stress halved, and VRP got worse anyway — falling implied with falling realized is not a seller's market, it is a market with nothing to sell. A first SELL_CPS still needs VIX above 16 for multiple sessions with VRP turning positive across a majority; today moved the other way on all three, and NKE at 65 is the entire case for tomorrow.

That gap is the whole story. One day of vol repricing lifts scores four points, not thirty-three; a first SELL_CPS needs this to persist long enough for base gate to stop culling nine of eleven, and today only establishes the direction, not the level.

**Notable:** The nine-session IWM streak is over: the tab's only durable name fails base gate outright and the board is a single line again, but the survivor is the other recycled name — **XLF at 0d, its fifth appearance and fifth zero-day print, score 30 (+3), on $0.17 credit over $0.50 width for $0.33 max loss.** The one genuinely new number is the skew: **c/w 34.0%, a run record for the tab and a full three points past XLF's own prior high of 31.0%, up from 17.0% four sessions ago** — so the skew clause of the old thesis suddenly has a carrier again, and for the first time in the run a ticket clears the 25% bar. Everything else fails it. **Score 30 is thirty-seven under the gate**, the credit is seventeen cents of absolute premium, RV status downgrades to **Caution** — the first non-Good/Excellent RV print of the run — and the funnel goes **checked 11 / actionable 1 / base_gate 9 / construction 1 / execution 0**, base gate now culling nine of eleven and execution empty for the **seventy-second straight print**. The surface ticks up without changing character: **VIX 14.43 → 14.92 off its run low, VIX3M 17.53, slope 0.771 — the flattest of the run; VVIX 86.6 → 86.3, essentially pinned**; contango intact, NORMAL. Regime re-labels back to **REGULAR SEASON at 0S / 0C with no watchlist at all**, and the internals deteriorate on both axes that matter: **avg VRP −5.6, 73% of 33 eligible negative (up from 69%), stress 12 (36.4%) versus 8 yesterday, danger 0**, RV accel 0.936 — stress broadening while VRP stays negative is the worst pairing for a premium seller. Day-over-day is broad but low-altitude: **XLB +20 off zero, SBUX +14, HD +13, TLT +9, XOM +8** against **XLV −14, XLI −13, NFLX −12, GS −6**, and the board tops at **XOM 42**, twenty-five under the gate, with XLF's 30 tenth on that list. **Still zero SELL_CPS at 72 sessions.** The read is cleaner than it has been in weeks: XLF proves rich skew alone doesn't produce a ticket, since 34% on a fifty-cent width is a rounding error of premium with a Caution RV flag attached. A first SELL_CPS needs the two halves to arrive together — record skew on a name that also passes base gate with real width — and with nine of eleven culled and VRP negative across three-quarters of the universe, that still requires a vol repricing, not a better day.

precisely the survival ceiling, XLE's mark, reached twice and fatal both times, so the count stands tied with its own record rather than through it.** The two ghosts die on schedule: **TLT and IWM, both back from retirement for exactly one session, both shed at 0d, the return lasting one print apiece — and IWM leaves the way XLE left, rising in the very session it is discarded (26 → 29, +3), the second time in five prints a carrier improves and the construction fails anyway, while TLT goes out at −4 and its second death lands ten sessions after the crown retired.** What replaces the three-ticket ledger inverts every number the record owns: **$2.33 of credit against $0.67 of max loss across $3 of width — c/w 77.5% against a headline that was 14.0% yesterday and never above ~17% before that, a 63.5-point jump from record low to record high in one session and roughly four and a half times anything the tab has ever priced — the first ticket in the count whose credit exceeds its own cliff, and by 3.5x: the largest credit ever carried sitting on the smallest max loss ever carried, where 08-07's fifteen cents rode $0.84.** The stamp column goes clean for the first time — **Excellent one-for-one, XLI's second consecutive Excellent, the first unanimous Excellent board of the record, and a note column flipping from three-for-three "Thin premium" to "High credit/width," the first time the notes have praised a ticket rather than flagged it** — but 77.5% of width on a $3 spread is not premium, it is location: nothing gets paid 78 cents on the dollar for selling calm, it gets paid that for standing at a strike the market prices as already reached, so **the compensation finally arrived and arrived in the wrong currency — intrinsic, not vol, in a print where carry is still −6.46.** The surface breaks all four of its floors at once: **VIX 14.63 → 14.25 (−0.38), a new all-time low 0.30 through the 08-12 record, the fourth 14-handle of the count and the first three in a row; VIX3M 18.61 → 18.46 (−0.15), through 18.53, its own record low; VVIX 89.4 → 87.5 (−1.9), a record low, the fourth sub-90 ever and third consecutive; contango 3.98 → 4.21, the first 4-handle the count has ever printed, past the record set two days ago — clean a twenty-fifth straight session (0 blocks), NORMAL** — and the cushion turns absurd against the cliff, **4.21 points of curve over $0.67 of max loss, 6.3x coverage where yesterday's three-ticket board had 0.57 and Friday's lone ticket had 4.5.** Beneath it every carry number backs off its record without changing sign: **avg VRP −7.11 → −6.46, off the first sub-−7 read ever and still the fourth-worst the count has measured; neg-VRP 77% → 63%, a 14-point collapse from record breadth, the largest one-session breadth improvement on record and still nearly two names in three negative; term slope 0.798 → 0.801, three thousandths back above the all-time low and still the second-flattest structure ever; stress 7 → 8 (23.3% → 26.7%); danger 0 a seventh straight; pool flat at 30 — and rv-accel 0.954 → 0.888, a second consecutive sub-1.0 and the deepest deceleration the count has recorded, realized now cooling rather than merely pausing.** The label reverts after exactly one print — **THE PLAYOFFS → REGULAR SEASON** — while the rail relights three-wide in the same session the board culls to one: **0S / 0C + 0W → 0S / 0C + 3W, the largest watchlist ever printed, tradeable still zero, pool and tab moving opposite directions a second straight session.** The mover tape widens to eighteen names, the most the record has shown, **12-up / 6-down, topping out higher than it has in seven sessions: JNJ 40 → 52 (+12) retakes the crown at the highest visible high since the record-low tops began, and 52 is still 13 points under the 65 score gate — a seventh consecutive session with no gate-clearer anywhere in a thirty-name pool** — with **MSFT 27 → 42 (+15) leading the ups, EEM 44 → 50 (+6) second on the tape, CAT +3 to 47, META +10, QQQ +7, EEM and GS +4/+6, SPY +5, HOOD +4, SBUX +3, XLE 27 → 30 (+3) visible and still unreadmitted; XOM 32 → 21 (−11) the day's largest fall, GLD −6, XLB −6 to 7, NFLX −4, TLT −4, XLF −3** — and the board's own carrier is absent from all of it: **XLI holds at 42 on a −1 too small to print, unseen on the tape in the session after posting the largest single-session rise the record has ever shown, the first time the tab's best riser was neither extended nor unmade in the following print, and the first time going quiet is what kept a ticket alive.** **Still zero SELL_CPS at 62 sessions**, and for the first time the standing trigger loses a clause instead of a carrier: **XLI's 77.5% clears the 25% skew gate by 52.5 points — the second clearance in the count's history, the first by any name but XLE, and by a margin more than five times the largest ever recorded — so the two-clause trigger becomes one clause on one name, binding constraint score alone: 42 against 65, 23 points, second-tightest board-high gap the count has measured behind yesterday's 22.** The case for it is banked and demonstrated — the c/w clause is no longer a distance but a possession, and the remaining 23 is smaller than the +33 this exact ticker printed two sessions ago. The case against it is everything else: **twelve clearances now and zero survivals; the only prior name to clear this gate was shed twice, both times at exactly the 1d XLI now holds; a board that went 3 → 1 in one print; and a compensation profile that reads backwards, record-wide curve and record-low front and record-low vol-of-vol paired with a credit that is large because the strike is close, not because the vol is rich.** Yesterday's reading was that the wall came down from the realized side; today realized cooled further and the board still shrank two-thirds, which sharpens that finding rather than reversing it — the gate is opening for a narrow, specific reason and closing on nearly everything that walks through. What is genuinely new is that the finish line changed shape: every prior print needed something to clear two gates and every clearance died there, and tonight one ticket holds one of them at 1d with the other 23 points away and a 33-point move already on its own record. The first SELL_CPS now needs two things it has never had at once — a 2d stamp, which nothing has produced since the Days crown retired ten sessions ago, and a score move smaller than one this very name has already made — and it needs them on a ticket whose entire edge is that it is standing close enough to the strike to be paid 77.5% of width for the privilege.

**Notable:** The wall that three identical prints proved load-bearing comes down in a single session, and it comes down from the side the thesis said couldn't move. **Checked 11 / actionable 3 / base_gate 7 / construction 1 — the first change in the funnel in four prints, the record's only digit-for-digit triple ending at exactly three, four names springing the first gate at once and the rail relighting three rungs where the count's only prior dark-rail exit (08-06) managed one: the first multi-ticket board since the eleven-session streak died.** The Days column, empty since Monday's shed, refills three-wide at absolute zero — **XLI 0d, TLT 0d, IWM 0d, aggregate inventory still zero ticket-days for a fourth straight print but with carriers under it for the first time since Friday** — and two of the three are ghosts: **TLT, the retired Days crown, walks back through the door nine sessions after the crown retired, and IWM returns from its own retirement in the same print, both at zero, both restarting from nothing.** The ledger they rebuild is the largest in weeks and the thinnest ever priced: **$1.05 of total credit against $6.94 of max loss across $8 of width — seven times Friday's fifteen-cent record-small footprint carrying eight times its risk — an average c/w of 14.0%, the first plural average since the streak era and a new record low under the 15.1% floor, dispersion reborn at 5.3 points (11.7% XLI / 17.0% TLT / 13.4% IWM), Thin premium unanimous three-for-three**, and a quality column that inverts 08-07 exactly: **Excellent / Good / Acceptable, one of each, the first Excellent stamp in seven lit boards, zero Caution where the last board was nothing else.** The surface barely moves and confirms everything by not moving: **VIX 14.55 → 14.63 (+0.08), the third 14-handle of the count and the first back-to-back pair, the record low effectively unrefunded; VIX3M 18.53 → 18.61 (+0.08), one tick off its all-time floor; VVIX 88.5 → 89.4, the third sub-90 ever and the first consecutive pair; contango dead flat at 3.98, both legs lifting eight hundredths in lockstep, still tied for the widest curve ever printed and 0.02 from a 4-handle — clean a twenty-fourth straight session (0 blocks), NORMAL** — and for the first time in the record the cushion outweighs the curve, 3.98 points of protection over $6.94 of cliff, 0.57 coverage where Friday's lone ticket enjoyed four and a half times. Beneath it the ledger splits cleanly in two, and the split is the whole story. The carry side sets three more records in the wrong direction: **avg VRP −6.85 → −7.11, a fifth record-worst in seven sessions and the first sub-−7 print ever; neg-VRP 70% → 77%, new record breadth, better than three names in four; term slope 0.854 → 0.798, through the 0.814 all-time low, the first sub-0.80 single-name structure the count has measured.** The realized side breaks the other way — **rv-accel 1.063 → 0.954, the first sub-1.0 read in nine prints, the eight-session acceleration streak dead; stress 16 → 7 (53.3% → 23.3%), better than halved in one session; danger 0 a sixth straight; pool flat at 30 — and the label turns over, REGULAR SEASON → THE PLAYOFFS, the pool changing seasons in the very print realized finally decelerates** — and that is what opened the door: the base gate is a realized gate, and four names cleared it because realized cooled, not because implied pays — the front rose eight cents, the curve didn't move a basis point, and the carry got a quarter-point worse. The rail columns swap sides to prove it: **0S / 0C + 1W → 0S / 0C + 0W, Wednesday's lone relit rung dead after exactly one print, the pool's tradeable count back to zero — while the tab goes 0 → 3 in the same session, the board fuller than the rail for the first time.** The mover tape prints fifteen names with a single decliner: **14-up / 1-down, JNJ 46 → 40 (−6) alone in retreat and surrendering the crown, CAT 28 → 44 (+16) taking the visible high at 21 points under the gate — a sixth consecutive session with no gate-clearer anywhere in the pool** — and the gain column belongs to the board's own anchor: **XLI 10 → 43 (+33), the largest single-session rise the record has ever shown, past SBUX's +22 and nearly double the +18 that stood as the best on-rail gain, printed by the name that was Tuesday's biggest corpse at −20 and landing it on the board in the same session it moved**; TLT +13 to 37 with its ticket, JPM +14, XLV +13, XOM +12, **XLE 15 → 27 (+12), back from unseen after exactly one session, the only skew-gate clearer in history returned to the tape but not readmitted to the board**, GLD +8, SPY +8, QQQ +7, GS +7, HOOD +5, PLTR +5, XLB +5 — with XLF and EEM, Wednesday's two leaders, going unseen in their turn. **Still zero SELL_CPS at 61 sessions**, and for the first time since Friday the standing trigger has carriers — three, with the clauses split across different names: **TLT stands 8.0 points from the 25% skew gate, nearer than either of XLE's 9.5 bookends and second only to the 5.0 that preceded the count's lone clearance, but 28 from the score gate; XLI stands 22 from the score gate — the tightest board-high gap since the cascade's first rung — but 13.3 from the skew gate; IWM trails both at 11.6 and 39.** What today adds is the thing no prior print contained: existence proofs for both clause-sizes on live paper — the skew clause has covered 12.5 points in two sessions before, and the score clause's required 22 is smaller than the +33 the tape just printed on the exact name that needs it, the first time a live ticket's remaining gate distance has been less than a move already demonstrated on that ticket. Against it stands everything the count knows: the gates have been cleared eleven times and survived never; the tape habitually unmakes its best riser in the next print and XLI is today's best riser by the widest margin ever; the survival record these three must beat is XLE's 1d, reached twice, fatal twice; and the compensation is the worst ever offered — record-low 14.0% average against record-worst −7.11 carry, the 08-07 rule at its terminal extreme. Yesterday's reading said the calm was why there was nothing to sell; today the calm deepened, the carry worsened, and three tickets appeared anyway — because the wall was never built on implied, it was built on realized, and realized just blinked. The door is open for the cheapest reason a door can open: not that selling this market finally pays, but that the thing being sold stopped moving fast enough to fail the gate — and whether a first SELL can ever fire now runs through tomorrow's stamp, where three tickets at 0d must first do what only XLE ever did, then what nothing has done since the crown retired, and then two record-sized things at once on the thinnest board the count has ever carried.

What today adds is the disproof of the last remaining hope: the surface has now made every concession available to it — record-low front, record-low three-month, record-low vol-of-vol, record-wide curve, all in one print — and the screen still cannot manufacture a candidate, because **cheap vol is the diagnosis, not the cure: at −6.85 VRP with 70% of the pool negative and realized still above 1.0, the calm is precisely why there is nothing to sell.** Monday proved the funnel could go solid, Tuesday proved the solid state was stable, and today proves it is load-bearing — three identical prints under the quietest tape ever recorded is no longer a screen refusing trades, it is a regime that has stopped generating them.

The regime prices the refund honestly: at −4.0 VRP the market's invoice for realized risk was 28%; at −6.1, the worst carry ever measured, the invoice is 15.5% — **the carry got worse and the compensation got thinner in the same print**, which is the one combination that cannot be traded at any size. The tab closes the week on one ticket-day, fifteen cents of credit and a 14-handle front, carrying the weekend on a rail whose entire population is a single WATCH nine and a half points from one gate and forty-two from the other — and the honest reading of a record-positive tape that produced no clearer is that everything rose and nothing rose enough.

For fifty-two sessions the tab couldn't fire because the skew was never there; today the skew nearly is, and the relit board's first act was to show why — the gate is finally within reach because, for the first time in the count, the trade on the other side of it is measurably bad.

The arithmetic of the subtraction is the session's core damage: **best c/w on the screen falls 17.0% → 14.8%, so the distance to the 25% skew gate re-widens 8.0 → 10.2 points**, the worst gate-distance since 07-22's 11.1, and the fourth consecutive time a shed has made the skew number *worse* — the screen has never once discarded a thin name. The surviving construction did fatten, and by almost nothing: **IWM $0.44 → $0.45 credit on an unchanged $3 width, max loss $2.56 → $2.55, c/w 14.7% → 14.8%**, a third straight positive per-ticket drift now measured at **+0.1, +0.2, +0.1 across three sessions** — and against a 10.2-point shortfall that pace implies a hundred sessions, which is the honest read of what "the tab has learned to hold inventory" is currently worth. Because the mechanism that was supposed to close the gap just failed its cleanest test. **Contango compressed 1.93 → 1.53, the narrowest print of the entire record**, past 07-20's 1.75 and 07-23's 1.90 — and it got there by a route the record has never taken: **VIX 18.58 → 18.67 (+0.09), VIX3M 20.51 → 20.20 (−0.31), VVIX 100.7 → 100.9 (+0.2), triple digits a third straight session** — **the first back-led compression of the count**, the long end doing three times the work of a nearly parked front, where every prior narrowing came from a violent front spike. Four readable sessions had lined up without exception on wider-curve/thinner-skew, and the last full point of narrowing bought **+2.5 points of average c/w**; today's four-tenths bought **+0.1 on the only ticket that survived to be measured**. The geometry didn't invert — it stopped paying, which is worse, because the trap it came packaged with is entirely intact: **1.53 is 1.53 from inversion and the plateau broke at 0.40 in a session**, so roughly four more prints at this pace put VIX through VIX3M and shut the screen mechanically, with **overlay clean an eleventh straight session (0 blocks)** right up to the edge. Beneath it the ladder redistributed violently: **base_gate 8 → 6, the one-session refill reversed straight back to the emptiest stack of the count**, and **construction 1 → 4, double the previous record bench**, four names one step from a screen that holds one — the deepest reserve and the thinnest screen the cycle has produced simultaneously, with **actionable 2 → 1, the column lit a fifth straight session**. Carry recovered without recovering: **avg VRP −1.17 → −0.85**, shallowing a third of a point off the second-deepest inversion of the record but sitting deeply negative for a second straight print, **neg-VRP dead flat at 48%**, still the widest since 07-17's 58%, on a pool holding at **21, the smallest of the recent record**, with **term slope re-flattening 0.95 → 0.917, the second-flattest read ever**, and **rv-accel 0.877 → 0.9, a twelfth straight sub-1.0 print** — realized still decelerating under a record-narrow curve, so the inversion remains single-name implied dumping on a quiet tape, and the c/w is now confirmed to be indifferent to it. The distress axis finally cleared: **danger snuffs 1 → 0 (4.8% → 0.0%), ending the longest lit run of the count at four sessions**, and **stress cools 6 → 4 (28.6% → 19.0%)**, a second straight easing off the record wide. The rail rotated but re-darkened at the top: **1S / 1C + 2W → 0S / 3C + 3W**, conditionals tripling and the watchlist adding a name, but **the outright-sell column dark again after exactly one lit session** — relit 07-24, out today, the same one-print cadence danger has been running. The label flips a **ninth time in ten sessions, REGULAR SEASON → THE PLAYOFFS**, elevated on snuffed danger, cooling stress and improving carry — the 07-22 paradox repeated verbatim, its key now demonstrably unreadable. And the tape ran quiet in a way it hasn't all cycle: **nine up, five down, and not a single vaporization or resurrection — no zeros printed, no name erased, every mover landing between 21 and 56**, the narrowest dispersion of the record one session after the triple wipe of MCD/CAT/XOM. **WMT's six-session climb through the 65 score gate died on schedule: 68 → 56 (−12)**, the fifth clearer in five to fail the two-print survival test (AMZN, NFLX, SBUX twice, now WMT), though notably the first to *retreat* rather than teleport to zero. **XLF −22 was the day's worst (43 → 21)**, a name that had spent three sessions climbing off the screen; **NVDA +15 (28 → 43) the biggest riser**, beside **NKE, XLV, TSLA +10 each, XLI +9 (36 → 45), QQQ +6 (44 → 50)**, against **XLE −7 (41 → 34)** and **EEM −5 (49 → 44)** — and for the first time in the count **the screen's own name is near the top of the tape: IWM at 51 is the second-highest visible score behind WMT's 56**, after twenty-odd sessions of the screen conspicuously ignoring the leaders. **Still zero SELL_CPS at 48 sessions.** The Days gate is not just met, it is three deep and has never been deeper. The overlay has never had less cushion. And the skew gate has moved *away* on three of the last four sessions — not because the surface refuses to print, but because the screen keeps subtracting the fat tickets and keeping the thin one that ages, and because the compression that was supposed to fatten it has now narrowed the curve to a record and paid a tenth of a point for it. What fires the first SELL is unchanged in name and worse in arithmetic: **IWM must add 10.2 points of c/w against a demonstrated drift of +0.1 per session, or a fat ticket must both reach the screen and survive a print** — something no name above 21.5% has ever done — **and both must happen inside roughly four sessions of contango at the current pace**. The tab now holds its deepest-ever inventory, its cleanest-ever distress reads and its emptiest-ever curve cushion, and the one number that has to move is moving in tenths.

Yesterday's verdict laid out two paths to the sequence: XLE survives and widens 3.5, or IWM reaches 2d and fattens 10.5. The tail did what it hasn't in five sessions: **0S / 2C + 3W → 1S / 1C + 2W, the outright-sell column relighting for the first time since 07-17** even as the rail thins. The label **holds — REGULAR SEASON prints back-to-back, just the second hold in ten sessions against eight flips in nine** — and it chooses to hold through the one input swing you'd have bet would flip it. What remains is one number and one now-thrice-measured behavior. **TLT at 1d needs 8.0 points of skew, IWM at 2d needs 10.3**, against a best per-ticket drift of +2.0 — four more sessions at a pace no ticket has sustained for even two — while the screen's filter keeps discarding exactly the fat-skew candidates the gate needs and keeps exactly the thin ones that age. Yesterday's verdict isolated the whole bottleneck to the single-name skew surface, its sharpest line being that the surface "has not produced a readable c/w in six sessions under every configuration the curve can offer"; today it produced one, and the answer the surface gave is **16.5%** — it can print, and when it prints it prints thin. And the geometry it printed under is precisely the anti-geometry the standing thesis has flagged all cycle: the front drained hard — **VIX 18.65 → 17.05 (−1.60), back beneath the 17.5-handle, VIX3M 20.40 → 19.59 (−0.81), off the 20-handle it first touched on 07-17, VVIX 102.8 → 96.3 (−6.5), back down through triple digits** — the front falling twice as fast as the long end, so **contango re-widened 1.75 → 2.54**, blowing clean through the two-session record-narrow plateau (1.77 → 1.75 → **2.54**) that yesterday's notable said the setup needed to *hold in the band* rather than extend or round-trip, and ironing straight back toward the wide flatten-skew inverse under which every skew flicker of the run has died. The c/w lit thin because the curve moved the wrong way to light it — the readable print and the anti-geometry arrived in the same session, which is the coherent, damning read: the skew surface produced its first number in seven sessions exactly as the index curve steepened away from the shape the gate wants. Beneath that the board did the rest by subsidence: **9 base_gate / 1 construction**, one name lost off the base floor as XLE climbed to the screen and the lone construction round held the bench a third-plus session without advancing — the actionable seat bought out of the base-gate stack rather than added to it, and **overlay clean a seventh straight session (0 overlay blocks) under a now-falling 96.3 VVIX**, contango intact, no mechanical trip. The carry column shallowed for a second straight negative print: **avg VRP −0.74 → −0.49, still inverted but a quarter-point less so**, the sign settling on the wrong side of the line rather than flipping (−1.12 → −0.74 → −0.49 now the tail of the whipsaw), with **term slope re-steepening 0.909 → 0.969** back toward 1.0, **rv-accel dead flat at 0.858 (from 0.857), an eighth straight sub-1.0 read** — realized still decelerating, so the inversion remains implied-drains-faster on a quiet tape — and **neg-VRP easing 45% → 43%** of a pool up 22 → 23, a minority now paying you to sell vol while the average reads negative, the damage still concentrated rather than broad. The distress axis, drained to a cycle low only yesterday, snapped right back wide: **danger re-lit 0 → 2 (8.7%)** after exactly one clean session, and **stress exploded 2 → 7 (9.1% → 30.4%)**, from the run's freshest low straight back toward its widest reads in a single print — the snuff-holds-one-relights cadence the count keeps confirming. The tail thinned where it had milled: **tradeable 0S / 1C + 6W → 0S / 1C + 3W**, the outright-sell column dark a third straight session, the six benched watchlist names halved to three — commitment neither built nor collapsed, just bled off the rail. The label flips on cue a sixth time in seven sessions — **THE PLAYOFFS → REGULAR SEASON**, calm today on a *falling* front and an *improving* carry, having been elevated yesterday on a falling front and negative carry — its key rewritten yet again. And the score tape ran a broad, orderly drawdown that is the exact inverse of yesterday's broad bid: twelve of thirteen movers lower, the lone riser **XOM +28 (0 → 28)** off the floor, against **XLI −14 (40 → 26) the day's worst and, tellingly, one of the three names ever to reach the screen surrendering its entire recent shelf, JPM −9 (32 → 23), NFLX −8 (39 → 31), JNJ −7 (41 → 34), UBER −6, MCD −6, NKE −6, SPY −5 (51 → 46), XLF −5, QQQ −4 (54 → 50, the highest visible score), GLD −3, EEM −3 (46 → 43)** — the median sagging while the extremes get gutted, no name anywhere near the 65 score gate. Of the trio that knows the way to the actionable screen, the split is stark and instructive: **XLE alone advanced onto the rail** while **EEM (−3, 43) drifted lower on the tape and XLI (−14, 26) collapsed** — the one name that reached the screen rose a single point as the other two fell, XLI hardest, the opposite of 07-16's unison climb. **Still zero SELL_CPS at 44 sessions** — and the verdict is the most concrete the cycle has produced, because for the first time two of the three standing requirements moved from theory to measured fact. A name reached the screen: met. A readable c/w re-printed: met — but at **16.5%, not the 25% the gate demands**, so the requirement narrows from "produce a c/w at all" (done) to "produce one **eight-and-a-half points wider than the first one the surface offered in seven sessions**," and it offered it under a curve widening away from the geometry that would fatten it. And the Days inventory did not lift: **XLE printed at 0d**, a fresh first-appearance, so the twenty-seventh straight zero holds intact — but that 0d is, for the first time in the entire count, a *concrete aging candidate* rather than an empty magazine, because a name is now sitting on the screen with a readable number attached. So the first SELL_CPS waits on the same two gates, now sharpened to specifics: **XLE (or any name) must re-print above 25% while contango survives** — today's 16.5% is the measured shortfall, and it came thin precisely because contango re-widened toward the anti-geometry — and **XLE must survive to re-print tomorrow to lift the Days floor off zero for the first time in twenty-seven sessions**, something no round has managed for even two consecutive prints in the whole run. The screen is no longer dark; it lights on a name at 0 days holding a c/w that undershoots the gate by a third, under a curve tilting back the wrong way — the closest the cycle has come to the sequence a SELL requires, and still two clean numbers away from firing it.

And the score tape printed its most violent churn of the count without touching anything that matters: **SBUX −73 (73 → 0), the high-water mark set only yesterday erased to literal zero in one session** — the highest score the cycle ever printed evaporating off the board exactly the way EEM's 45.5% did, the run's signature move now confirmed at both ends of the tape, records set and vaporized inside twenty-four hours — alongside **MSFT −53 (53 → 0), HOOD −41 (41 → 0), META −29 (29 → 0)**, four names gutted to the floor at once, against a distinct financials bid on the other side, **GS and JPM surfacing from nothing at +22 apiece (0 → 22) with XLF +3 (17 → 20) beside them**, plus **TLT +7 (24 → 31), CAT +5 (41 → 46) taking the visible-tape lead, GLD +4 (38 → 42), XLV/XLI +3**, versus **AAPL −6 (51 → 45), UBER −5 (49 → 44), SPY −4 (42 → 38), NVDA −4 (41 → 37), WMT −3**; most tellingly, **AMZN — which only yesterday became the first name on the CPS side ever to clear the 65 score gate, at 67 — is absent from the movers tape entirely**, the gate-clearing print neither extended nor visibly revoked, in limbo on the one axis that had been advancing; and of the three names ever to reach the actionable screen, **XLI alone resurfaces (+3, 34 → 37)** while **XLE and EEM stay off the tape a third straight session**. **Still zero SELL_CPS at 40 sessions** — and today's verdict is a freeze over a deterioration: the machine did not move a single round in either direction — same magazine, same bench, same dark screen — while the conditions beneath it surrendered Tuesday's entire recovery, carry re-inverted deeper than Monday, breadth back to sixty-one percent against, the slope re-flattened, the contango re-widened to second-widest of the cycle on a fresh vol slide, with only the danger snuff and a one-tick stress cool breaking right. What's missing is unchanged in kind and now unchanged even in posture: **a c/w has to re-reach the 25% skew gate** — no readable skew has printed for three sessions, and the geometry that must deliver one, a front-led steepen that *holds*, has now round-tripped twice into its exact inverse — and **the Days inventory has to lift off the zero it's held twenty-three straight sessions**, which a board that didn't move one round cannot begin to do. The pattern hardening around those two gates is the whipsaw itself: carry flipping sign three times in four sessions, the curve setting records in both directions inside a week, the tape's high-water marks — EEM's 45.5%, SBUX's 73 — printing and vanishing on consecutive days, everything around the binding axis oscillating violently while the binding axis has not produced a single day of inventory in twenty-three sessions. Loaded at one bench round and frozen there: the CPS tab neither advances nor retreats today so much as it holds still while the ground tilts back the wrong way beneath it — both hard gates exactly where they've stood, carry back on the wrong side of the line, the anti-geometry re-asserted at fresh lows, and the first SELL_CPS still waiting on the same two numbers no amount of churn around them has ever moved.

But the round that finally chambered live chambered into the WATCH breech, not the firing chamber: **XLE prints c/w 18.5% — 0d, score 30, $0.19 credit on a $1 width, $0.81 max loss, RV Good** — a live, readable skew at last, 2.8 points richer than 06-27's 15.7% and the best the cycle has ever surfaced, yet still **6.5 points thin of the 25% gate**, so the machine surfaces a WATCH rather than a SELL, a round in the chamber that can be *read* for the first time but not yet *fired*. And beneath the live read the Days gate holds exactly where it has sat eighteen straight sessions: **XLE at 0d**, nothing above it, the Days inventory pinned at absolute zero even now that a round has reached the screen — the second structural blocker stacked under the skew shortfall, an actionable name that clears construction and still cannot fire because it carries neither a 25% c/w nor a single Day. The catalyst extended its front-led lift a second straight session, and in the right shape again — **VIX 16.13 → 16.90 (+0.77), VIX3M 19.01 → 19.46 (+0.45), VVIX 87.9 → 91.4 (+3.5)** — spot vol up better than three-quarters of a point off Monday's cycle floor, vol-of-vol vaulting back above 90 to 91.4, and the front rising better than half again as fast as the long end for a second running, so **contango compressed again 2.88 → 2.56 (VIX 16.90 vs VIX3M 19.46), a second consecutive front-led compression off the 3.21 fourth-widest record**, term slope steepening a second straight session **0.931 → 0.978 toward 1.0** off 07-06's flat floor, rv-accel easing **1.063 → 1.032** — the exact front-led steepening the 25% c/w skew gate has demanded for weeks now printing two sessions in a row, the curve un-bending from the maximal flatten-skew inverse and carrying XLE's c/w from an implied-nothing to a live 18.5% as it goes, and this time holding rather than round-tripping the way 07-01's long-end bounce did the very next day. The carry column held the right side of the line a second session — **avg VRP +0.29 → +0.35, positive two straight after three sessions beneath** — but the breadth crept the wrong way, **neg-VRP 45% → 54%, back above the split**, a slim majority of the now-26-name eligible pool tipping back to paying you *not* to sell vol even as the average stayed positive; against that, the regime's outright sell re-ignited off zero, **tradeable 0S / 1C+2W → 1S / 1C+1W**, the lone SELL that had dropped clean to zero on 07-07 back on the board beside a conditional and a watchlist name. The score tape re-rated as always and, for the first time in the count, cleared its own ceiling — **SBUX +6 (60 → 66), the first score anywhere above the 65 gate since the count began**, though on a non-CPS name and on the score axis, not the binding c/w axis, the two axes as independent as ever with the highest score of the cycle sitting nowhere near the screen while the actionable name carries a score of just 30, a full 36 points beneath it — ahead of **AMZN +9 (50 → 59), NKE +13 (21 → 34), PLTR +10 (28 → 38)**, and, most tellingly, **XLI — the only other name that has ever reached the actionable screen — climbing a second straight session (26 → 40 → 47), back toward the bench XLE just cleared**, the two names that have ever chambered live both turning toward the screen at once, against a hard give-back — **GOOG −38 (38 → 0), XOM −30 (46 → 16), TSLA −22 (22 → 0), XLB −18 (18 → 0) all gutted or off the board**. The regime context held **REGULAR SEASON** a third session, and the distress columns stayed curdled — **danger held at 1 (3.8%)** and **stress at 14 of 26 (53.8%), still better than half the pool flagged**, the cluster the standing thesis wants extinguished neither cooling nor spreading but holding elevated beneath the breakthrough. **Still zero SELL_CPS at 35 sessions** — but today is unambiguously the least-dark session of the entire count and its first genuine structural advance: the magazine at its fullest, a live round chambered onto the actionable screen for the first time in the run, the first readable c/w since 06-27 and the richest the cycle has printed, vol geometry front-led and steepening two sessions running, carry positive two straight, the outright sell re-ignited, the score gate cleared. What's missing is now narrowed to exactly two hard numbers on one name: **XLE's c/w has to climb the last 6.5 points from 18.5% to the 25% skew gate**, which the front-led steepening now delivering two straight sessions has to keep delivering rather than stall; and **the Days inventory has to lift off the zero it's held eighteen straight sessions**, which a 0d WATCH cannot do on its own. Around those two, the softer conditions that turned right must hold: the front-led compression has to keep leading up rather than round-trip to a fresh low; carry has to hold positive a third session and drain neg-VRP back under the split rather than let it creep further above; the distress cluster has to finally re-cool; and the three construction rounds stacked behind XLE have to keep advancing to the screen the way it just did rather than fall back to the bench. Loaded at last with a live round — the first the count has ever chambered — but chambered into the WATCH breech six and a half points and a Days-lift short of the firing chamber: the CPS tab has its breakthrough session, a name off the floor, through construction, onto the screen with a readable skew, and stands closer to a SELL than at any point in the entire count — yet still not close enough to fire, the live round in the chamber a WATCH and not a SELL until the c/w clears 25% and the Days gate lifts off zero.

And the carry column, frozen two sessions at its cycle-worst −1.4, crossed decisively back the right way: **avg VRP −1.4 → +0.29, the first positive print in three sessions**, nearly a full 1.7 points reclaimed in a single session, with **neg-VRP draining 69% → 45%** — off the cycle high and back beneath the split, the 29-name eligible pool no longer a two-thirds majority paying you *not* to sell vol but tipping back toward paying you to. The regime context, though, cut both ways under the turn: label steady at **REGULAR SEASON**, but the CPS-relevant sell thinned rather than thickened — **tradeable 1S / 0C+1W → 0S / 1C + 2W**, the lone outright sell that had stood alone for sessions dropping clean to zero even as a conditional and a second watchlist name surfaced beneath it — and the distress columns turned squarely wrong: **danger re-ignited 0 → 1 (3.4%)** after two clean sessions, and **stress ticked to a fresh high 14 → 15 (48.3% → 51.7%)**, now better than half the pool flagged, the cluster the standing thesis wants extinguished instead curdling further. **Still zero SELL_CPS at 34 sessions** — but today is unmistakably the least-dark session of the entire count: the magazine at its fullest, the vol geometry front-led and steepening for the first time, carry back positive, neg-VRP drained under the split, XLI turning back toward the bench. What's still missing, though, is exactly the pair of things that most directly gate a shot: both construction rounds have to *clear* the 25% c/w skew gate rather than die on the bench, which the front-led steepening that finally arrived was not yet enough to force; and the Days inventory has to lift off the zero it's held seventeen straight sessions, which a pair of 0d construction rounds cannot do. Around those two, the softer conditions turned mostly right and must now *hold*: the vol bounce has to keep leading front-first rather than round-trip to a fresh low the way 07-01's long-end bounce did the very next session; carry, having crossed back positive, has to hold rather than flip a fourth time; the distress cluster has to re-cool rather than curdle; and a name — XLI or another — has to hold onto the screen it keeps fading off. Loaded, and for the first time loaded twice over, but still unfired — two rounds chambered and both blanks, the catalyst finally lifting the right end first and the curve finally bending the right way, yet the skew gate uncleared and the Days gate un-lifted: the CPS tab has its most constructive tape of the cycle and is still dark in the two columns a SELL would need lit most, closer to firing than it has stood in weeks and still not close enough to chamber a live round.
